Details of the report you made concerning the strange lights that you saw in the
sky recently have been passed to this office, as we co-ordinate reports of UFO
sightings in this country. Our only concern is to establish whether or not
there is a threat to the security of the United Kingdom. Unless we find
evidence of such a threat - and this has not been the case so far ~- we do not
attempt to investigate further, or to identify what was seen.

While it is clear from the reports we receive that there are many strange things
seen in the sky, we believe that explanations could be found for most of them.
Tf you believe that what you saw cannot be explained in terms of known objects
or phenomena, you might like to contact some of the civilian organisations
turrently engaged in the study and investigation of UFOs. These organisations
may be able to offer some further thoughts on what you sav, or tie in your
sighting with other reports they may have received. I suggest the following

groups:

As promised, I have looked into the question of what the paperwork
detailing the UFO sighting reported to RAF Leeming MiSection 40. |
Unfortunately, I have been unable to find any record that the report ever
reached us.

I was contacted by a number of researchers about a wave of sightings that
occurred in the Teeside/Cleveland area on the 23, 24 and 25 March this year. I
was also asked about a story that two aircraft from RAF Leeming were diverted
from a routine training sortie to an area where a UFO had been sighted, and I
assume this vas the report made + SEED I spoke to authorities at RAF
Leeming about this at the time, and they confirmed that nothing was picked up on
radar either at RAF Leeming or at Teeside airport. With regard to the aircraft,
no diversion took place, although it transpired that the flight paths had taken
the aircraft through the area where the UFO had been reported, at the time
concerned. The crews were subsequently asked if they had seen anything unusual,
but they had not.

T hope this is helpful.

Yours , sec ,

—

Details of the report you made concerning the strange object that you saw in the
sky recently have been passed to this office, as we co-ordinate reports of UFO
sightings in this country. Our only concern is to establish whether or not
there is a threat to the security of the United Kingdom. Unless we find
evidence of such a threat - and this has not been the case so far - we do not
attempt to investigate further, or to identify what was seen.

While it is clear from the reports we receive that there are many strange things
seen in the sky, we believe that explanations could be found for most of them.
If you believe that what you saw cannot be explained in terms of known objects
or phenomena, you might like to contact some of the civilian organisations
currently engaged in the study and investigation of UFOs. These organisations
may be able to offer some further thoughts on what you saw, or tie in your
sighting with other reports they may have received. I suggest the following

groups:

British UFO Research Association Quest International

Contact International (UK)

I hope this is helpful.

Our reference
D/Sec(AS)12/3

/

As promised, the following paragraphs set out our policy and views on the UFO
phenomenon.

Although the Ministry of Defence does receive reports of UFO sightings, our only
concern is to establish whether or not there is any threat to the security of
the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that there is - and this has not been the
case with any sighting to date - we do not attempt to investigate further, or to
identify whatever might have been seen.

Given this limited involvement with the subject, the Ministry of Defence has no
department which is appointed solely for the purpose of studying UFO reports,
nor are there any staff employed on the subject full time. The reports we
receive, which are usually limited to very brief details of what was seen, are
passed to staff in departments concerned with the air defence of the United
Kingdom, who examine them as part of their normal duties.

It is clear from the reports we receive that there are many strange things to

be seen in the sky. However, we believe that explanations could be found for
most of them. Some of the possible explanations include aircraft lights or
aircraft seen from unusual angles, airships, satellites in orbit or satellite
debris re-entering the atmosphere, high altitude kites, helium balloons, weather
balloons, searchlights or lasers reflecting off clouds, ball lightning,
Fireballs and meteorites. Additionally, some reports turn out to be hoaxes.
Having said this, we accept that there will always be some sightings that appear
to defy explanation, and we remain open-minded about these.

The Ministry of Defence does not release material on the subject "en bloc",
because, as is the case with all government departments. all Ministry of Defence
files are subject to the Public Records Act, and generally remain closed from
public viewing for 30 years after the last action has been taken. Prior to 1967
most UFO files were routinely destroyed after about five years, because at the
rime there was not perceived to be sufficient public interest in the subject to
warrant the permanent retention of this material. A small number of earlier
files did survive, and may be viewed at the Public Record Office. The
references of these files are as follows: AER 16/1199, ATR 20/7390, AIR 20/9320,
AIR 20/9321, AIR 20/9322, AIR 20/9994 and PREM 11/855. This final file contains
a letter on UFOs from the then Prime Minister, Winston Churchill, together with

the response.
ORY.
rey

Recycled Paper
I have had a recent look at the situation, and have discovered that because
Files were not destroyed immediately, surviving papers generally date from
around 1962. The reason these papers have not yet been released is that they
are in Files where the last enclosures are more recent, and files can only be

“released when the most recent énclosure is 30 years old. The good news is that,

although it is not our practice to comment in detail on material that has not
yet been released, I understand that a further two files (Public Record Office
reférences AIR 2/16918 and AIR 2/17318) are due for release in January 1994.

- Prom then onwards there should be a steady trickle of UFO files released each

year: If you are interested in seeing these files, I suggest that you. contact
the Publie-Record Office early next..year-

The files we hold in this office go back to about 1985; so we are able to answer
specific queries. that people have about particular sightings which -have occurred
in recent years, and can let them know whether we have a report on the incident,
and if so, what it contains. Most of the reports that we have are relatively
unexciting, and refer to little more than a vague shape or light in the sky, as
opposed to the more exotic "close encounter" reports that are sometimes written
about. For whatever reason, people seem to report these cases to UFO groups or
researchers as opposed to us. I have attached for your information a copy of
the form that we use to record UFO reports. :

You may be interested to know the numbers of UFO sightings reported to the
Ministry of Defence in the past, so I have attached some figures that you may
find useful. “

A number of recent reports were almost certainly sightings of an airship, with
its main body brightly illuminated. I have seen this myself, and can understand
how, when seen. from a distancé, this generated. reports of a bright cigar-~shaped
UFO. Other tecent reports have been caused by the use of lasers or
searchlights; such equipment is used increasingly in advertising and
entertainment, especially at outdoor music festivals.

The Ministry of Defence is not involved in any research or investigation into
the crop circle. phenomenon, although we occasionally get questions on this
subject, because of the perceived link between crop circles and the UFO
phenomenon. Although having no responsibility for this subject, we will refer
anybody who asks about them to organisations such as CPR International or the
CCCS, in the same vay as we will refer people who report. or enquire about UFOs
to groups such as BUFORA or Quest International. As is the case vith UFOs, we
hold no fixed views on crop.circles, and remain open-minded about. this
intriguing phenomenon.

Any requests for MOD participation in a documentary must be made. through the

Defence Pres ffice (RAF), who are in Room of Main Building, and may be
contacted on Section 40 ;

I hope this is helpful, and has explained our position. Please let me know if
vou have any questions. : :

Your reference

D/Sec(AS)12/3
Date

21 October 1993

Thank you for your letter dated 15 October in which you raised a number of
points relating to the UFO sighting at Rendlesham Forest in 1980.

Firstly, you asked about the release of UFO reports, and_asked if this would not
have breached the "30 Year Rule". I believe that what was
referring to was the release of specific UFO reports. The release of an entire
file would have breached the rules, but releasing some material from a file
would not. What we have said is that if people are enquiring about specific
sightings that have occurred recently, we will look through our file, to see if
we have any reports that might tie-in with what was seen. If we do, we will
release a copy, although we delete the names and addresses of the witnesses,
together with any personal information, in line with our rules on witness
confidentiality. This system works well, and is a good example of how we assist
serious UFO groups and researchers. :

You asked for copies of material sent to Section 40 am unsure what, if
any, reports we sent her, although any such reports would have been released
under the terms of the arrangement described in the previous paragraph. In any
case, it would not be proper for me to release details of correspondence between
her and us to any third party, without permission, just as I would not pass
details of our correspondence to any third party. This approach mirrors our
rules on witness confidentiality.

With regard to your point about whether or not the Rendlesham Forest sighting
posed a threat to the defence of the UK, the fact is that we have no evidence of
any threat. You should bear in mind that although stories persist about
structured craft and aliens, Lt Col Halt’s report mentions nothing more than
lights in the sky, together with some other observations that - as the article
jin "Focus" suggested - might be very easily explained.

Il can make no comment about what was said about UFOs being related to stress;
this is clearly one theory, and there may or may not be any truth in it. As TI
explained in my previous letter, our role is very limited; we do not take sides
in the wider debate about the UFO phenomenon, and remain open-minded.

You mentioned the audio recording allegedly made at the time of the UFO
sighting. I have heard the tape, and my personal view is that the tape was a
hoax (hoaxing is, unfortunately, extremely widespread where UFOs are concerned).
Having said this, ufologists are divided on this point. Even if the tape is
genuine, it in no way substantiates any of the more exotic stories that are
circulating about this sighting. Clearly, however, you will wish to listen to £AY¥
the tape yourselves, and make up your own minds. I believe that Quest
International have copies of the tape.

Recycled Paper
Although I have not seen a copy of the covering letter you mention, it is not
unlikely that one was sent, and I imagine that it amounted to little more than a
note drawing our attention to Lt Col Halt’s report. As for the other papers,
drawings and photographs you mention, I know nothing of these. Many strange
stories circulate about this UFO case; our problem is that they are not
supported by any hard evidence.

I hope this is helpful.

Details of the report you made concerning the strange object that you saw in the
- esky on Tuesday evening have been passed to this office, as we co-ordinate

reports of UFO sightings in this country. Our only concern is to establish
whether or not there is a threat to the security of the United Kingdom. Unless
we find evidence of such a threat - and this has not been the case so far ~ we
do. not attempt to investigate further, -or to identify what was seen.

While it is clear from the reports we receive that there are many strange things
seen in the sky, we believe that explanations could be found for most of them.
If you do believe that what you saw cannot be explained in terms. of known

objects or phenomena, you might like to contact some of the civilian
organisations.currently engaged in the study and investigation of UFOs. These
organisations may be able te offer some further thoughts on what you saw, er tie
in your sighting with other reports they may have received. I suggest the
following societies:

British UFO: Research Association west In

Contact International (UR)

vel vo.

I hope this is helpful.

Staff at RAF Finningley have told me about how you ~ together with other staff
and pupils at St Peter’s -~ saw a strange object in the sky on Wednesday 6
October. My job includes dealing with questions about UFO sightings, although
our role is very limited, and our only concern is to see if there is any
evidence of a threat to the United Kingdom. Unless we find evidence of such a
threat ~ and this has not been the case so far - we do not attempt to
investigate further, or to identify what was seen.

While it is clear from the reports we receive that there are many strange things
seen in the sky, we believe that explanations could be found for most of them.
Aircraft seen from unusual angles, or large airships are two very common
explanations. If you do not think that what was seen could be so easily
explained, you might like to contact some of the UFO groups, who might have some
other ideas. I suggest the following societies:

British UFO Research Association Quest International

Contact International (UK)

I hope this is helpful. Please let me know if I can be of any further
assistance.

Details of the report you made concerning the strange lights that you and other
witnesses saw in the sky on Friday night have been passed to this office, as ve
co-ordinate reports of UFO sightings in this country. Our only concern is to
establish whether or not there is a threat to the security of the United
Kingdom. Unless we find evidence of such a threat - and this has not been the
case so far ~ we do not attempt to investigate further, or to identify what was
seen,

While it is clear from the reports we receive that there are many strange things
seen in the sky, we believe that explanations could be found for most of them.
T£ you do believe that what you saw cannot be explained in terms of known
objects or phenomena, you might like to contact some of the civilian
organisations currently engaged in the study and investigation of UFOs. These
organisations may be able to offer some further thoughts on what you saw, or tie
in your sighting with other reports they may have received. I suggest the
following societies:

Thank you for your letter dated 2 October, copied to the Secretary of State for
Defence. Please treat this reply as the response to both letters.

I was interested in the information that you provided, but I have yet to see any
official documents relating to these UFO sightings. We have no record that the
Belgians (or anybody else) passed us any information relating to these
sightings, and I can only assume that the appropriate military authorities did
not believe that there was any threat to the UK.

i can assure you that the Air Defence of the UK is taken very seriously; you may
recall from the days of the Cold War that Soviet aircraft used to test our
defences on a regular basis, by attempting to penetrate the UK Air Defence
Region. You may also recall the very effective way in which the RAF detected
and intercepted these aircraft.

Your reference

Our reference
D/Sec(AS)12/3

Date
11 October 1993

. =

In my letter dated 5 October I explained that the only reason the MOD looks at
UFO sightings at all is to see whether there is any threat to the defence of the
UK. When we spoke on Friday you asked me who evaluates the sightings, and how
judgements are made about whether there is or is not any threat.

Sightings are evaluated here in Sec(AS)2a, and we are assisted by specialist
staffs whose responsibilities cover the Air Defence of the UK. It is not our
policy to name other departments involved, but I can tell you that the staffs
concerned examine UFO reports routinely, as part of their normal duties. There
is no department which is appointed solely for the purpose of studying UFO
reports, nor are there any staff employed on the subject full-time.

In order to say that there was a threat, we would have to be presented with firm
evidence; to date, we have not been presented with any. Most of the reports we
receive are quite vague, and relate to little more than a distant light or shape
in the sky; furthermore, we often get to hear of a sighting some time after it
has occurred. In these circumstances, there is very little that we can do. If
we have something more interesting (such as a sighting with more than one
witness, or very precise details of a sighting) then we can make some checks,
such as finding out whether there was any military aircraft activity that might
explain what was seen.

I should tell you that, for whatever reason, people seem to report alleged
"close encounters" to UFO groups as opposed to us.

I can assure you that all UFO sightings - together with any other UFO-related
data that is sent to us - are carefully looked at, in an open-minded way.

You also asked me to expand on my comments relating to cooperation between the
MOD and UFO groups and researchers.

I believe that cooperation is important, and helps everyone involved with
investigation of UFOs. The main form that cooperation takes is the exchange of
information; ufologists will often send us cases and ask us for any views we may
have. In turn, we will suggest that witnesses get in touch with UFO groups, and
are therefore responsible for getting a lot of sightings into the public domain.
We also release general information, such as the data I passed you on the number
and distribution of UFO sightings reported to the MOD.

eb

Recycled Paper
You also asked if I could give you any further details of sightings in the
Bristol area. Recently, we have received the following reports:

"Sometime in August" ~ Lockleaze area
23 August - Bedminster area

28 August - St Georges area

26 September -— area not known

6 October ~- Frenchay area.

When you called today, you asked me to say something about the term "UFO". In
that UFO simply stands for Unidentified Flying Object, we entirely accept that
there are such things - clearly people often see something they are unable to
identify. The term "UFO" does not imply that what was seen was
extraterrestrial, and as I explained, we have no evidence that would support the
existence of extraterrestrial life. As I have said before, although we believe
that most UPO sightings can be explained in terms of known objects or phenomena,
we accept that a small Percentage would appear to defy explanation. We remain
open-minded about these.

Finally, as promised, please find attached the Civil Aviation Authority report
into the airmiss incident that you mentioned. .

I hope this information is helpful.

PART A: SUMMARY OF INFORMATION REPORTED TO JAS

THE B737 PILOT reports that he was inbound to Gatwick and in communication with London control on 128.4
while squawking with Mode C. He was heading 308° and descending through FL 140 at 355 kt with visibility in the

région of 40 km. His FO saw a small black lozenge shaped object about 500 m ahead of the ac and at the same
less than 100 yd away, down the port side of the ac and,

feyel, Within the space of about 1.5 sec it passed very close,
d London Control, via the RT, who reported seeing a

at the most, 30 ft above the level of the wing. He informe:
small primary radar contact astern of the ac. No impact or disturbance was felt on the ac and no damage was

observed during a post flight inspection. He assessed the risk of collision as high.

ATC AT LATCC reports that during a-moderate traffic loading on the BIG/EAS CCF sector the B737 pilot
reported an object passing his ac in the Opposite direction at a high closing speed. The B737 was inbousd to
Gatwick via the Eastwood stack and passing the BEXTL way point. He was descending through 15,000 ft and
thought the object may have been a balloon, or part of, describing it as black and about 18 inches wide. The SC
reports that a primary return was seen about 10 NM behind the B737 heading SE.at about 100 kt. A following ac
was given traffic information and avoiding turns 10 the left to avoid the primary return, which had appeared ta
changé heading towards it, but its pilot reported seeing nothing, ‘The DVR CSC was also advised of the facts in

order that inbound traffic could be given vectors to avoid the area.

JAS Notes

085.76? and at a range of 30 km from Crawley. It ascended through 15,000 ft 13 min after release ( 1732) when it was
bearing 097.6° and at a range of 17 km, There was a sharp inversion between 4000-5000 ft where the temperature
rose from +8* to +12°. The top of convection was at 4500 ft, above this was a very dry area up to about 15,000 ft.

2. AIS(Mil) undertook very thorough tracing action but ¢
did show a primary return in the general area but it was seen Lo move about like a light ac or helicopter at low level.

No Met or other balloons could be traced which having been released could have reached this area/time nor which
could bave appeared black. The only possible exception to this was that some hot-air balloon pilots launch small
balloons which can be followed with a device called a "Wind Tracker’. One balloon pilot contacted had released
balloons on the 6th/7th July but then not again until the 19th. On the 21st he tracked one of his black balloons to
7200 ft before he lost sight of it and be assumed they could go much higher than this. The CAA SRG is well aware
of this use of balloons; they and others of similar size (less than 2 m) are exempted from most restrictions of the

ANO by Article 109(a). The object seen by the B737 pilot must be considered as untraced.

A member commented that the number of sightings of ballooas and other unidentified objects seem to have become
more prevalent inthe § and SE of the country. Members took note of the AIS(M) comment on the primary return
seen in the general area of the airmiss as being more akin to the movement of a ght ac or helicopter at low level.
They were at a loss to identify the reported object since its shape and behaviour did not seem to fit any recognisable
piece of aviation equipment, while the temperature inversion made it unlikely that something like a piece of black
polythene could have been carried to that level. Ir was agreed that this airmiss was best described as a confliction
between the B737 and the unidentified object. Whilst members were unsure what damage could have occurred had
the object struck the B737 the general opinion was that there had been a possible risk of collision.

Thank you for your letter dated 6 October.

I am afraid that it is not our policy to discuss the distribution of UFO data
within the Ministry of Defence, beyond saying that Sec(AS) are assisted by
specialist staffs with responsibilities covering the Air Defence of the UK.
These staffs examine such data routinely, as part of their normal duties. Their
task ~ like ours - is to look for evidence of any threat to the UK. I£ no such
evidence is found, no further action is taken.

I hope this is helpful.

Yours soucerel,

6 October 1993

I'm writing to you to enquire if you could answer a query I
have re. the UFO subject (again!). I frequently file Freedom Of
Information requests with various branches of the U.S.
Government for access to UFO-related files and have -just
received a newly-released document from the Defence
Intelligence Agency (copy enclosed). As you will see from the
distribution list a copy of the document was sent to the
Defence Intelligence Staff (DIS) of the Ministry Of Defence. In
view of this would it be possible for you to answer me the
following questions:

1. Does DIS routinely receive copies of similar data from the
U.S. Government?

2. Did DIS forward a copy of the enclosed document to your
department?

3, As DIS were recipients of this document does this mean that
DIS conducts its own investigations/evaluations of UFO reports
or would they have taken no action ?

Should you be able to assist me re. the above I would be very
glad to hear from you. Thankyou for taking the time to read my
letter. I have also forwarded a copy of the enclosed document
to as he told me that he would be seeing you
shortly.

Yours Sincerely,

Thank you for your letter dated 4 October, and for sending me a copy of your
paper on declassification of official US documents.

You asked if I could let you know of any UFO-related points of interest; I can
tell you that so far this year we have received 176 reports, and I have attached
a copy of the map showing the distribution of these sightings. We are aware of

quite a few sightings in the Bristol area, but many are thought to have been
generated by lasers or searchlights, and sightings of Venus rising.

I hope this is helpful.

“In 1972-a programme of "SYSTEMATIC REVIEW FOR DRCLASSIFICATIONS
began, Under this pra

ramse classified, permanent: Ly vad
farchival] records are reviewed for the purpose of declassifica~
tion. This programe is applicable bo those records which have
rqached a specific age Executive Order 12356, authorised NA-
Lignal. Archives and Records Administration as the sole agency re-
quired> to conduct a systematic review of ite enormous classified
holdings;, NARA’s implementation of this programe is a duplicate
of its British counterpart - Publie Records Office [PRO], which
applies Public records Act. NARA like PRO reviews - with an  in-
tention to declassify records - when they are 30 years old. Cer-
tain intelligence or cryptologie files series require a longer
period of 50 years, before their eligibility for declassifica~
tion.

While other agencies are not required .to conduct a systematic
review progvanme, from now on they are veneourated todo so, They
ean now, with the approval of the originator, agencies, ineluding
NARA, conduct a systematic review of records that are less thon
30 years old.

Unfortunately, both the number of pages reviewed andl declassified
in 1992 decreased significantly. The US agencies reviewed 10.7
million, pages, almost & million [82%] less than in 1991. Only 9.4
pillion pages were declassified. Agencies declassified 88% of the
page reviewed. Approximately the same proportion declassified in
1991.

Although the decrease in systematic review is due ta the low
figures reported by several agencies, NANA’s figure critically
impacted on the aystemabic review product. In 1092, NARA’s reviow
decgeased by: 2 million pages to 6 million, adrop af 28% from
1991. WNARA’s explanation for the deduchion in ite systemabic
review yere a} the absence of sufficient staff bo fulfil the
programme; and .b} the reeords reviewed included a smaller per-
centage of classified documents within the aggregate files.

The Department of Defense ‘TDOD} accounted for the second highest
volume of pages reviewed in 1992. Despite this factor, the number
of pages reviewed and declassified \by DOD ‘decreased sig-
nificantly. It dropped from 6 million in 1991, to, almost 4 mil-
lion pages. in 1992. The reasons provided by DOD were the

MANDATORY REVIEW

Under the provisions of the Executive Order 12356, the mandatory
review process permits the agencies, or the eitimzens for that
matter to require an agency to review specified national security
informataon,:. seeking its declassification. Mandatory Review is
popular with some serious researchers as a less contentLious al-
bernative to Freedom of Information Act [FOTA]. It is one of very
few ,vehicles allowing to.seek the declansification of presiden-~
tial’ papers or records, which are excluded from the FOTA.

Mandatory Review Pages Processed 1993 1992
Granted ‘in Full : 66, 203 LOL, 14
Granted in Part 64,222 118,
Denied 13,2204 8,7:
Yotal "439,205 233,693 _

In 1992, 3867. cases were processed under the mandatory review.
Comprising of 57,000 documents, amounting to 233,693 pages. The
total munber of pages processed showed an increase of 68% to the
previous year - 1991. The rate of pages’ declassified in whole or
in part also increased by the same rate - 68%. US Government
maintaing that Mandatory Review is a highly successful means for
the declassification of information.

Executive Order 12356, also enables the public researchers or or-
ganisations ‘to appeal mandatory review denials to designated of--
ficials of the denying agencies, In 1992, 229 such appeals were
filed by public researchers and various organisations. As result,

4605 documents, totalling 15,608 pages were reviewed by the Ap-
pellate authorities. Of these, 98% of the pages were declassified

in wholetor in part. Which tends to suggest that researchers can
anticipate more. returns in declassified informalion i4€ they
pursue ar appeal. Equally significant is that as declassification
decision reaches’ to ai higher level within an agency, the
likelibood of declassification expectedly is greater.

DERLVATIVE. CLASSIFICATION

Derivative Classification is the act of incorporating,
paraphrasing, resting, or generating in new form classified in-
formation. - Information in this category could be classified in
the fol lowing two forms; : :

1) Through the use of a source document* - normally correspon—-
dence, or publications generated by an original olassification
authority. -

2) Through the use of Classification Guide. A Classification
Guide is a set of instructions issued by an original classifica-
tion authority. Lb is usually limited to a specifie, or par-
ticular subject: “By deseribing the elements of information
therein, highlighting the information that wust be classifieds

work to restate classified source information, can classify
derivatively.

7 October 1993

Thank you for your letter dated 19 September to the Prime Minister, concerning
the 1980 UFO sighting at Rendlesham Forest. I have been asked to reply.

While we are aware of Lt Col Halt’s memo, I am afraid that we have no other
papers to hand. The files for 1980 are - like all government files ~ covered by
the "30 Year Rule", and will not be open to the public yet. I can tell you that
Lt Col Halt’s report was examined very carefully at the time, and that we vere
satisfied that there was no threat to the defence of the UK.

As you may be aware, there is a lot of controversy about this UFO sighting. You
may have read about it in "Skycrash", by Jenny Randles, or “Above Top Secret",
by Timothy Good. There are many, however, who believe that there may be a
simple explanation for what was seen. You might like to buy the latest issue of
the magazine "Focus", which (as part of an article about UFOs) looks at the
Rendlesham Forest case, and concludes that the lights seen were from Orford Ness
lighthouse! Potential explanations are also put forward for the other things
that Lt Col Halt found, such as the holes in the ground.

Although this is clearly a fascinating mystery, our interest in UFOs is very
limited. If you are interested in the subject, you might like to write to the
organisations that actively research and investigate UFOs. I suggest the
following groups:

British UFO Research Association uest International

Contact _International (UK)

I hope this is helpful.

Yours Simenel, ;

Bear Sir,

I am writing to you,te ask if you could please anwser my
questions. My questions go back 13 years to 1980,a year before we were
born. Over a period of a week between Christmas and New Year, 1980, a
series of mysterious events changed the lives of Police and civilians, an
UNIDENTIFIED CRAFT was sighted near Rendlesham Forest, England. The case
involved the U.S. and U.K. N.A.T.O Forces. A Commanding Officer of the
U.S. Forces reportedly made contact with the aliens, photos were taken
(photos were taken suggests that more than one roll of film was taken. )
Films were made (were made, maybe this means recorded on video tape, of
photo film was taken.) high radiation levels were obtained (maybe this
padiation level of 6.1 milliroentgens were obtained from the U.F.O. landing
place (or did it crash.) in the centre of the three depressions [the
readings were Beta, Gamma.]} trees were damaged, and the lives of many
people shattered...

This remarkable event did occur, believe it or not! and I do not expect the
Government or you Sir to deny it because the official report was released
by the Department of the Air Force, New York to the R.A.F. on the i3th. of
January 1981 under the freedom of information act.

The event took place in East Anglia, Suffolk at Oxford Ness near the
towns/villages (which-ever} of Gxford and Sudborne. The U.F.O. came from
the east/north east of R.A.F. Bentwaters and it crashed about 11), miles
sectio 4 the east gate of R.A.F. Woodbridge. It was first sighted by

over his house in Sudborne and then headed onto the B.A.F.base.
Dear Sir, I request that, if possible, you disclose as much information as
possible about this incident and I am sure if you require more information
yourself, then you can obtain it from the R.A.F. Tf hope you will reply to
US aS Soon as possible. Thank you for your time.

Yours faithfully,

1. Early in the morning of 27 Oec BO (approximately 0300L), two USAF
security police patrolmen saw unusual lights outside the back gate at

RAF Woodbridge. Thinking an aircraft might have crashed or been forced
down, they catled for permission to go outside the gate to investigate.
The on-duty flight chief responded and allowed three patrotmen to ¢ra-
ceed an foot. The individuals reported seeing a strange gtowing object
in the forest. The object was described as being metalic in appearance
and triangular in shape, approximately two to three meters across the
base and approximately two meters high. [t qlluminated the entire forest
with a white light. The object itself had a pulsing red bight on top and
a bank(s} of blue lights underneath. The object was hovering or on legs.
As the patrolmen approached the abject, it maneuvered through the trees
and disappeared. At this time the animals on a nearby* farm went into a
frenzy. The object was briefly sighted approximately an hour later near
the back gate.

2. The next day, three depressions 1 1/2" deep and 7” in diameter were
found where the object had been sighted on the greund. The following
night (29 Dec 80) the area was checked tor radiation. Geta/ganma readings
of 0.1 milliroentgens were recorded with peak readings in the three de-
pressions and near the center of the triangle formed by the depressions.
A nearby tree had moderate (.05-.07) readings on the side of the tree
toward the depressions.

3. Later in the night a red sun-like light was seen through the trees.

It moved about and pulsed. At one point it appeared to throw off glowing
particles and then broke into five separate white objects and then dis~
appeared. Immediately thereafter, three star-like objects were noticed

in the sky, two objects to the north and one to the south, all of which
were about 10° off the horizon. The objects moved rapidly in sharp anguler
movements and displayed red, green and blue lights. The objects to the
north appeared to be elliptical thraugh an 8-12 power Jens. They then
turned to full circles. The objects to the north remained in the sky for
an hour or more. The object to the south was visible for two or three
hours and beamed down a stream of light from time to time. Numerous indivi-
duals, including the undersigned, witnessed the activities in paragraphs

2 and 3.

Vow

When we spoke earlier today I promised to let you have photocopies of the maps
that we produce showing the approximate location of those UFO sightings reported
to the Ministry of Defence in recent years. I have attached these maps,
together with a table showing the numbers of reports we received.

You asked for any comments I may have about the recent wave of UFO sightings in
the Bristol area; I am aware that some of the sightings are thought to have been
caused by people having seen Venus, and that others were probably caused by
people having seen lasers or searchlights reflecting off clouds; I know that
BUFORA and Quest International have received a lot of sightings, and have looked
into the situation; you may wish to liaise with them. As I mentioned,

rom DUFORO has also done a lot of research into the recent Bristol

sightings.

I am not suggesting that all the recent UFO sightings in the Bristol area were
caused by Venus or lasers/searchlights; as I mentioned, one lady reported that
"sometime in August" she saw "a pen shaped object" which disappeared and
reappeared three times before finally moving out of view. She described it a
being black in colour with areas of chrome, and said that it was silent. The
sighting occurred in the Lockleaze area.

I thought it might be useful if I set out our policy and views on the UFO
phenomenon; the following two paragraphs set out our position.

While the Ministry of Defence does receive some reports of UFO sightings, our

only concern is to establish whether or not they pose a threat to the security
of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this has not been the
case so far, we do not attempt to investigate further, or to identify whatever

might have been seen.

It is clear from the reports we receive that there are many strange things to be
seen in the sky. However, we believe that explanations could be found for most
of them. Possibilities that spring to mind include aircraft lights or aircraft
seen from unusual angles, kites, helium balloons, weather balloons, unusual
cloud formations, satellites in orbit or satellite debris entering the
atmosphere, ball lightning, fireballs and meteorites. We accept, however, that
there will always be some sightings that appear to defy explanation, and we
remain open-minded about these.
&
Recycied Paper
I entirely agree with your comments about cooperation between the Ministry of
Defence and ufologists; we always try to be as helpful as we can, and will do
our best to answer questions about specific sightings, together with more
general questions on our policy and views on UFOs.

I hope this is helpful. Please let me know if you have any further questions.

UFO SIGHTINGS INCLUDING THE RAMSEY AREA REPORT

1. Thank you for your letter regarding my Working Paper 12/93 and the matter of reporting in
any UFO reports. In the case of the Ramsey sighting the NCO i/c the PBX saw the UFO phone call as
an ideal opportunity to check out his automatic recording facility, designed with bomb and terrorist
threats in mind rather than UFOs, and consequently the interesting report was taped.

2. Having spoken to various people on the unit there seems only to be a vague awareness of some
reporting procedure. Three days on from raising the query no one has come up with copies of those
‘standard forms' you referred to. Disseminating the details of correct procedures to all the places you
mentioned would be rather expensive. Perhaps alternative reporting methods should be considered,
such as a free phone number to an answer phone with a taped set of instructions for reporting in the
salient points required ?

3. The business of JARIC being involved was a misunderstanding. It was thought, that with
JARIC on stand down at the time the Ramsey report was phoned in, the Research Branch was asked to
help instead of referring it to JARIC. In fact the Research Branch was chosen by PBX staff to look
into the report and your concept of JARIC only being involved to comment upon specific photos,
film, etc. is almost certainly right.

4, I regret now being quite so specific in quoting only Alconbury when the alternative American
bases of Mildenhall and Lakenheath might have been the parent base for some training operations.
This would allow Alconbury to disclaim responsibility with a clear conscience ! Your premise of
another type of helicopter is questionable since very few helicopters I can think of would have that
long term hovering capability, operating with high power settings and consequent high fuel
consumption.

5. Amusingly enough the area of the sighting, in the Fenland district, is likely to evoke comments
to the effect that the local population (the Fen Tigers) wouldn't really notice a few aliens unless the
abnormalities were fairly marked, like having two heads. Derogatory local humour.

6. Hope these further comments have been useful.

SSO
Res Sc la

Thank you for your letter dated 22 September.

You asked about the "close encounter” cases that we released last year.
Following a number of enquiries, I wanted to put into the public domain those
very few cases that we had which went beyond the sighting of lights or vague
shapes in the sky. Following a search of the files held in this office, I
identified five such cases,- and released them to the national UFO groups, having
deleted the names and addresses of witnesses - together with other personal
details - because of our rules on witness confidentiality. I sent BUFORA’s
copies to the ERIS address, but given that they seem to have gone

astray, I have attached copies of these cases.

In line with our rules on witness confidentiality it is not our practice to give
details of exchanges of correspondence or meetings with other ufologists. We
are happy to meet serious researchers in order to explain our policy and views
on UFOs, and have done so on a number of occasions.

I was interested in your views on the Halt tape; clearly you have done a lot of
research into this. Given the time that has now passed, and the fact that the
personalities involved have moved on, I suspect it will be impossible to resolve
the mystery. I have made it clear that my view that the Halt tape is a fake is
a personal one. The objections that occurred to me as being ones which cast
doubts on the tape’s authenticity were points that I thought would be useful to

researchers.

I entirely agree with your comments about cooperation between the Ministry of
Defence and ufologists; we always try to be as helpful as we can, and will do
our best to answer questions about specific sightings, together with more
general questions on our policy and views on UFOs.

I hope this is helpful.

Our reference

D/Sec(AS)12/3
Date
29 September 1993

Thank you for your letter dated 27 September.

I agree that radar will only positively detect a radar wavelength or an object
of opacity and substance, although it is important to remember that a radar blip
does not necessarily correspond to the location of an object; as I explained in
my letter dated 18 February, the phenomenon of Anomalous Propagation can give an
indication that an object of some sort is in the air, when in fact there is no
such object; the radar will be picking up a natural feature such as a coastline.

i am not aware of instances where radar operators have detected an object,
judged it to be solid, and not been able to identify it; if there have been ‘such
cases then they probably relate to weather balloons. ‘The point I was trying to
make in my 18 February letter was that we have never detected a structured craft
flying in UK airspace, that has remained unidentified.

Although, as you point out, there is a chain of radar stations stretching across
NATO, it is not the case that they all automatically exchange data, so it is not
correct to say that the Ministry of Defence must have been aware of radar
detections that occurred during the 1989/90 UFO sightings in Belgium. When I
said, in my letter dated 24 February, that I remembered only one reference to
radar sightings, I made it quite clear that this was a personal recollection of
comments made in. Timothy Good's book, "Alien: Liaison".

I really must stress again that while the sightings that you are researching are
doubtless very interesting, they occurred outside UK airspace, and as such lie
outside our remit.

I wish you the best of luck with your continuing attempts to get to the bottom
of this mystery.

Yours sucertd

29 September 1993

Your letter concerning the strange object that you saw in the sky earlier this
month has been passed to this office, as we co-ordinate reports of UFO sightings
in this country. Our only concern is to establish whether or not there is a
threat to the security of the United Kingdom. Unless we find evidence of such a
threat - and this has not been the case so far - we do not attempt to
investigate further, or to identify what was seen.

While it is clear from the reports we receive that there are many strange things
seen in the sky, we believe that explanations could be found for most of them.
if you do believe that what you saw cannot be explained in terms of known
objects or phenomena, you might like to contact some of the civilian
organisations currently engaged in the study and investigation of UFOs. These
organisations may be able to offer some further thoughts on what you saw, or tie
in your sighting with other reports they may have received. I suggest the
following societies:

British UFO Research Association Quest International

Contact International (UK)

I hope this is helpful.

Yours sinh ;

Details of the report you made concerning the strange object that you saw in the
sky on Saturday night have been passed to this office, as we co-ordinate reports
of UFO sightings in this country. Our only concern is to establish whether or
not there is a threat to the security of the United Kingdom. Unless we find
evidence of such a threat - and this has not been the case so far - we do not
attempt to investigate further, or to identify what was seen.

While it is clear from the reports we receive that there are many strange things
-seen in the sky, we believe that explanations could be found for most of them.
I£ you do believe that what you saw cannot be explained in terms of known
objects or phenomena, you might like to contact some of the civilian
organisations currently engaged in the study and investigation of UFOs. These
organisations may be able to offer some further thoughts on what you saw, or tie
in your sighting with other reports they may have received. I suggest the

Thank you for your letter dated 20 September.

* am not able to shed any light on the jets that were seen in the vicinity of a
UFO on 4 September. I am aware that a colleague checked to see whether any
military aircraft were in the area at the time, and that no record of any such
activity was found. There are no further checks that I can make.

You asked again about our views on the UFO sightings on 30/31 March; I can
confirm that these sightings remain unexplained (but not necessarily
unexplainable - an important distinction). However, our only reason for looking
at UFO sightings at all is to look for evidence of any threat to the UK, and as
{ explained when you telephoned yesterday, we could not say that there was a
threat until we had positive evidence. To date, we have received no such
evidence, and given that this is the case, I do not believe there are any
grounds for us to carry out further research or investigation. I can assure
you, however, that all UFO reports are carefully studied, and that we are

open-minded in our approach.

I was interested to hear about the spate of UFO sightings in the Taunton area on
3 August, and, as promised, I have attached a copy of the report we have that
might tie-in with these sightings. I have deleted personal details of the
witnesses (a husband and wife) in the usual way, but have written to them
explaining your interest, and inviting them to contact you if they so wish. I
have attached a blank copy of the form we use to take details of UFO sightings,
to assist you in interpreting the report I have sent.

Finally, when we spoke, I promised to send you an address for NASA, so that you
can ask for some information about SETI. The address is as follows:

HQ Information
NASA
Washington DC
20546

USA

It is my understanding that all SETI data will be made available to the public.

REPORT OF AN UNIDENTIFIED FLYING OBJECT

1. Date, time &
duration of sighting

2. Description of object
(No of objects, size, shape,
colour, brightness, noise)

3. Exact position of observer
(Indoors/outdoors,
stationary/moving)

4. How observed (Naked eye,
binoculars, other optical
device, camera or camcorder)

5. Direction in which object
first seen (A landmark may be
more useful than a roughly
estimated bearing)

6. Angle of sight (Estimated
heights are unreliable)

7. Distance (By reference to a
known Landmark)

8. Movements (Changes in 5, 6 & 7
may be of more use than
estimates of course and speed)

9. Met conditions during observations
(Moving clouds, haze, mist etc)

10. Nearby objects (Telephone lines,
high voltage lines, reservoir, lake
or dam, swamp or marsh, river, high
buildings, tall chimneys, steeples,
spires, TV or radio masts,
airfields, generating plant,
factories, pits or other sites with
floodlights or night lighting)

To whom reported (Police, military,

11.
press etc)

12. Name & address of informant

13. Background of informant that
may be volunteered

14. Other witnesses

15. Date and time of receipt

16. Any unusual meteorological

conditions

INVESTIGATION REPORT Case No: 935 & 935A

Case Name or Reference; ‘Axminster & Packsaddle Sightings’

Introduction

Although the following reports occurred on different dates they
are considered to be sufficiently similar in nature to warrant
them as a dual event, possible of the same object.

The first event (Case No 935) occurred on the 14th May 1993 and

SEO Axninster, Devon. This is an older style terraced

cottage, virtually in the centre of Axminster. Because of its
lecation; the rear and only garden of the property does not
afford its occupants a very good view of the surrounding area.
At 10.40pm on the day in question EERMMmmghad gone out in the
garden to smoke a cigarette and upon looking up noticed a
bright light approaching from the S.W. He estimated its height
to be between 10,000' and 20,000‘ and at an elevation of about
75 degrees to the horizon. On its approac escribes its
speed as faster than a jet aircraft and on a consistent course.
As the object reached a position right above his garden - it
suddenly stopped. This action rather startle qiaieleg as
although he was unable to identify the object he really hadn't
considered it to be anything other than perhaps a high flying
aircraft or even possibly a satellite.

The object remained stationary for for a few seconds, then
performed a forward motion going into a anti- clockwise circle
and returning to its former position.

{2
(2)
After another slight pause, the object then shot forward approx
2 or 3 miles and again stopped abruptly. It then performed
another circular motion stopping at its second position and
after a short pause returned to its approx first position over
the garden. At this point it once again performed another
circular motion and again stopped dead. It then moved forward
very fast and disappeared towards the S.E.- ascending very
fast. Duri i light from its first stationary position to
its ‘cee creo to his wpersection 40) fiat came out and
witnessed the second and final movements of the object and its
departure.
Both witnesses described the object as about the size of a new
five pence piece at arms length and agreed that the object
appeared oval in shape and although described as a bright self
luminous object appeared to be tinged with an orange / reddish
colour. Nei theo EMM ould identify the object, but
they are certain it was not an aircraft or a helicopter.
In all the sighting lasted approx 1% minutes and needless to
say left both witnesses more than a little puzzled.

This sighting occurred on the 6th July 1993 and it has many
similarities to the Axminster sighting. My investigation into

this sighting has been mainly by telephone, but with the added
advantage of seeing BERIRNROMEEN and his family being interviewed
on local TV concerning this event.

As with HERRON sighting ad gone outside into his

back garden for a smoke. It was 11.18pm and being a clear night
SceHG was taking in the late evening sky. Suddenly he became
aware of a brightly lit object appearing over the top of his
house and moving very fast. It was on a S.E. course and at a
great height. He estimated the distance of the object from him
to be a ‘few miles' and at an elevation of 60 degrees to the
horizon.
Whilst watching the objects progress, it suddenly came to a
stop and remained stationary for a few seconds. It then moved
forward in a zig-zag movement, for approx 20 seconds and then
descended very fast towards the S.E. almost as if it was
heading towards the moon! ‘Section 40

Bot son and his friend have verified the sighting and

/6
(6)
Conclusions
From the descriptions of these sightings, it is fairly obvious
that what was seen on these two nights was not a conventional
aircraft or helicopter. No aircraft to my knowledge, and this
includes the Harrier Jump Jet, can suddenly stop in mid air
from this sort of speed. In addition, I don't believe that an
aircraft could perform the type of aerobatics described by the
witnesses. Because of the sudden stopping of these ‘crafts’ it
is also reasonable to assume they were not Satellites.
Fireballs, Bolides or any other known natural phenomenon.
This being the case - what were they?
They can of course be simply classified as ‘Unidentified Flying
Objects’ - as this they surely were, but into what category do
we place them.
Are they ‘alien space ships' piloted by ‘alien beings’ or some
advanced form of air/space craft developed here on earth?
Maybe the answer lies somewhere between the two!
During the last couple of years or so, there has been much
speculation about the existence of captured ‘Flying Saucers’.
this obviously relates to the so called Roswell incident of
1948. If, and I only say if, the USAF is in possession of some
alien space craft, then it is quite possible that they have
been subjected to some very intensive back engineering and as a
result are now being flown by them. It's also possible, I
suppose, that these craft could be piloted by their original
crews ~ but I think that’s best left well alone for the time
being.
The reason for this speculation is of course the stories
emanating from America and the now infamous Area 51 scenario. I
recently obtained a tape recording, made in the USA from a
program called ‘For the People'. On this tape some well
intended and apparently well informed person, is heard
disclosing recent events and some film footage taken of these
events over Area 51 (Groomlake) which depicts brightly coloured

/7
(7)
objects rising from behind some mountains and traversing the
sky at great speed. They are then seen to hover and move
forward in a zig-zag motion before moving away at great speed.
If these reports are genuine, then this must open up a debate
as to whether these objects are being flown outside the US and
if so, are they being seen in the skies over Britain.
Both the objects mentioned in this report were seen to approach
from the N.W.,which may indicate that they came from across the
Atlantic - travelled north of Ireland before crossing the S.W.
of England.
As stated earlier this is only speculation, but without further
evidence as to the objects origins, make-up or mode of
operation, there is little else to go on.
There is no doubt in my mind that the reports given pg cco]
Section 40} and are genuine, and that what they witnessed
cannot be explained by any known natural phenomenon.
The ariel performance by these objects is to say the least
quite remarkable, and if they do turn out to be some form of
advance air/space craft made on this earth, then perhaps the
billions of dollars being spent on the so called ‘Black Budget‘
may some day be justified!

Section 40} 3rd August 1993

INVESTIGATION REPORT - CASE NO: 938

Gase Name or Reference ‘ Taunton / St Agnes Sightings’
(August 1993)

Introduction

Although two days apart both these sightings are so similar in nature and
description that there is undoubtedly a connection between them.

Both concern the sighting of 'two cigar shaped objects' traversing the
skies above Taunton, Somerset and St Agnes, Cornwall, in what appears to
be an identical flight pattern.

The first sighting was personally reported to me Mymsection40, | who
lives in Taunton: is an employee in one of the shops, within the
shopping centre that

Knowing of my interest in ufos, she related her sighting to me,
appreciating that if nothing else she would not be ridiculed or thought
of as a fool etc. I have known or the best part of three years and
have always considered her to be an honest and trustworthy person.
because of this, I believe her sighting to be quite genuine, and can
actually corroborate part of her report myself - which is as follows:-

At approx 8.i5am on Saturday 7th August 1993 Fee. as standing at a bus
stop near her home, awaiting her bus to take her to work. She was alone
and on looking up at the sky noticed a rather extraordinary number of
aircraft vapour trails. Although it is not unusual to see vapour trails,
it did seem to her that there was rather a lot of them and more
importantly they appeared to be criss~crossing the sky - every which way.
Their configuration reminded her of a ball of wool having been undone and

/2
(2)

left untidily laying about. Whilst looking at them she noticed an
aircraft travelling northwards and heading away from Taunton. As the
aircraft disappeared from sight, she then saw two cigar shaped objects,
one following the other, moving very slowly across the sky in a northerly
direction. Because of their speed she was able to watch them for nearly
five minutes. She described them as silver in colour and reflecting the
early morning sun. They appeared to be about 30' apart and at a height
slightly lower than the aircraft previously seen. She estimated each
object to be about 14" in length - at arm » They were at an
elevation of about 70° to the horizon, eee unable to judge
their distance from her. At approx 8. 20am FEO) bus arrived and she
then lost sight of the objects.

On this particular morning, I arrived in Taunton at approx 7.55am and
whilst parking my car, I happened to look up at the sky and noticed the
rather Large number of vapour trails. Being in the west,, the sky above
Taunton, particularly on saturdays, does get very busy and vapour trails
are not uncommon. However, on this morning there was an extraordinary
number of them and were as I would expect them to be mainly from east to
west ( west to east), there were a number that criss-crossed the sky and
indeed some seemed to have approached from one direction and joined then
followed some others. The impression I got, was that a number of
aircraft had converged on one another and then headed away northwards!

The second sighting came from a police officer - Section 40,0

serves with the Devon & Cornwall Police Force. At ipm on Tuesday i0th
August 1993, EEO Fiance)and her sister Section 40 a
driving along the B3285 east of Goonhavern, Cornwall, when they noticed
to the S.W. two objects flying or moving S.Easterly. They appeared to be
about 5 or 6 miles away and in line, one behind the other, moving very
slowly and appeared to be on a predetermined cour se FEMI) described
them as silver / white in colour and at arms length about the

/3
(3)

size of a cigarette. She estimated that they were between 45° to 60° to
the horizon and that they (Section 40 FRR her sister) had the objects in
sight for for about ten minutes. This obviously indicates a fairly slow
speed, and if SER OM estimate of the objects lengths are correct, then
they must have been fairly large!

Investigation and Conclusions

Because of my very heavy work load (Business and UFOs), I have not been
able to investigate these sightings in any great depth.

Fortunately, because of my close work association with Section 40) | I
have been able to interview her firsthand. From her report and from what
she has told me of the event, I feel that what she saw on this morning
was certainly out of the ordinary. She is, I'm sure, able to identify
aircraft when she sees them and other natural aerial objects such as -
Hot Air Balloons, Helicopters, Gliders etc. Given the distance of the
objects from her, it is conceivable that they may have been some type of
aircraft! If they were, they would most probably have been some
military type flying in formation and also quite possibly carrying out a
re-fueling operation! This is of course merely speculation on my part,
but could I believe be one explanation.

What concerns me or rather I should say intrigues me, is the
extraordinary number of vapour trails that morning. Only last week, 1
received a report of the sighting of a single cigar shaped object over
Bristol on Saturday 4th September 1993. The object was travelling very
fast in an easterly direction and a few seconds after being seen, a
number of fighter aircraft (7 or 8) were also seen. The jets arrived from
all compass points and converged east of Bristol and appeared to be
chasing the Ufo! Inquiries to the MOD (Air) of course produced no
results, but this is being followed up by letter. (Case No 93'3 to
follow, refers).

/4
(4)
Both ladies are adamant that there were no ‘wings' or other ‘appendages'
affixed or associated with these objects, which does indicate that they
were not any known conventional aircraft. Given the spate of ufo
sightings in the west county over the last few months, it is reasonable
to assume that these objects may have been ufos of some description or
other.
Although all the objects were ‘cigar shaped’ there is of course the
possibility that they were in fact ‘disc shaped’ and seen from an angle
or side view. Again this is speculation, but as the object seen over
Bristol on the 4th September was observed in a ‘vertical’ mode and not
‘horizontal', then all things are possible.

In conclusion, although I have to say that there is the possibility that
these objects may have been aircraft, and if so, probably military, I do
feel that what was seen on these two days and also on the 4th September

over Bristol, were definitely unidentifiable by the sighters, and must,

for the time being anyway, be classified as ‘unknowns’.

ection 40, 17th September 1993

Thank you for your letter dated 17 September, in which you asked about
official interest in the crop circle phenomenon.

As you suspected, there is no real interest in crop circles. We do co-ordinate
reports of UFO sightings in this country (where our role is to check that there
is no evidence of any threat to the UK - no such evidence has emerged to date),
so because of the perceived link between these two phenomena, we do get
enquiries from the public about crop circles from time to time. Having said
this, we have no formal remit on the subject, and have never carried out any
research or investigation. We will refer members of the public who ask about
crop circles to organisations such as the Centre for Crop Circle Studies and
Circles Phenomenon Research International. We have also told these
organisations, together with UFO groups such as BUFORA, that we are interested
in seeing the results of serious research into the crop circle phenomenon. Our
reason for doing this is to enable us to deal with public enquiries in an

informed way.

Although I have heard the story about alleged discussions between the Ministry
of Defence, the Department of the Environment and the Ministry of Agriculture,
Fisheries and Food, I am not aware that any meeting ever took place. It may
well be that discussions took place between officials who had received letters
from the public, in an attempt to decide who should reply! I am aware that two
Parliamentary Questions have been tabled by MPs on the subject, and I have
attached copies of the relevant extracts from Hansard.

I hope this is helpful, and I wish you the best of luck with the video. Please
let me know if you have any further questions.

: Helicopters .

88, Sir Michael McNair-Wilson: To ask the Secretary
of State for Defence what low-flying restrictions are
imposed on service helicopters.

Mr. Neubert: The general regulations governing the use
of the United Kingdom low flying system by fixed-wing
aircraft apply also to rotary wing aircraft.but with
appropriate modifications to take account of their
different capabilities. o .

* Railgun

90. Mr. Wallace: To ask the Secretary of State for

Defence if he will report on the Progress of the Railgua
Project.

Mr. Sainsbury: I refer to the replies that I gave to the
hon. Member for Clackmannan (Mr. O’Neil) on 28 June,
column 456,

Independent European Programme Group

92. Mr. Ian Taylor: To ask the Secretary of State for
Defence what progress is being made through the
Independent European Programme Group in achieving a
more open defence market among North Atlantic Treaty

Organisation allies.

Mr. Sainsbury: Significant progress is being made by
the Independent European Programme Group towards
the creation of an open European defence equipment
market, based on measures set out in the action plan
approved by Ministers at the Luxembourg meeting in
November 1988. At their subsequent meeting in Lisbon on
28 June this year, Ministers reviewed the steps being taken’
to achieve the key objectives. The central feature of the
open market is the dissemination to potential suppliers of
information about bidding opportunities. To this end,
Governments have now nominated one or more focal
points to which companies in other Independent European
Programme Group countries can register interest in
bidding for defence contracts. Countries have also agreed
to publish contracts bulletins by the turn of the year. The
bulletins will be similar to those already issued by the
United Kingdom and France. This more open market will
bring benefits to all participating states. It will also be of
considerable value to the North Atlantic Treaty
Organisation as a whole by enhancing efficient procure-
ment and thereby strengthening the* European
contribution to the Alliance. With this in mind the
Independent European Programme Group will continue
to discourage any protectionist tendencies on either side of
the Atlantic. Another important initiative which comple-
ments the open market is the creation of a European
technology plan——to be known as. Euclid—which wil!
foster co-operation in research..among Independent
European Programme Group nations. During its current
chairmanship of the Independent European Programme
Group, the United Kingdom’s aim will be to ensure that
the work of the group is taken forward effectively in the
best interests of all the allies.

Toxic Waste

Dr. Thomas: To ask the Secretary of State for Defence
if, pursuant to his reply to the hon. Member for Newport,

356 CW93/40 Job HEY

11 JULY 1989

Written Answers 498

West (Mr. Flynn), Official Report, 19 June, column 60, on
toxic waste at Ministry of Defence premises, he will give
the reason why it is not the practice to release details of
such materials: and if he will consider revising his policy
towards publication in line with the practice adopted by

United States military facilities.

Mrs. Wise: To ask the Secretary of State for Defence
what is the basis for his policy not to disclose details of
radioactive and toxic waste stored or disposed of at
Ministry of Defence premises.

Mr. Sainsbury: Disclosure of such information could
indicate the scale’and ature of operations in certain
programmes on which detailed revelation would not be in
the national interest.

Fuel Containers (Devon)

Mr. Speller: To ask the Secretary of State for Defence
if he will make a statement on the circumstances in which
two 10ft x 2ft fuel containers were dropped at Stowford
Cross in North Devon on Thursday 29 June; and what
steps he is taking to prevent this happening in the future.

Mr. Neubert: The “fuel containers in question were
jettisoned by a Royal Navy Sea Harrier operating from the
Royal Naval air station at Yeovilton, while the aircraft
was on an air combat training exercise. The aircraft
unintentionally went into a spin and the pilot, following

’ the correct procedures, released the fuel tanks as part of his

spin recovery action. The fuel tanks were empty at the
time. A local investigation has established that the pilot
was not in breach of any regulations.

To minimise the risk to the public, air combat training
is carried out over the least populated areas available and
always well away from towns and cities. It is also very rare
for a Sea Harrier to enter into a spin in this way. The risk
therefore of a repetition of this incident or of any
consequent injury to persons on the ground is considered

to be remote. :

initiated by Army helicopters based in the south-west in
investigating the origin of flattened circular areas of wheat;
and if he will make a statement.

Mr. Neubert: . The Ministry. of Defence is "not
conducting any inquiries into-the origins of flattened
circular areas of crops. However, we are satisfied that they

are not caused by service helicopter activity. -

DEFENCE
Kurt Waldheim

Mr. Andrew Mitchell: To ask the Secretary of State for
Defence when he will publish the results of the review of
the 1986 investigation into the fate of captive British

- servicemen and the possible involvement of the then

Lieutenant Waldheim.

. Mr, Archie Hamilton: My right hon. Friend the Prime
Minister announced on 16 February 1988 that a review
would be undertaken of the results of the investigation
carried out in 1986 by the Ministry of Defence into the fate
of British servicemen and the possible involvement of the
then Lieutenant Waldheim. This was in the light, of both
the report of the Commission set up by the Austrian
Government, which had access to a wider range of sources
than those available for the 1986 investigation, and of the
continued questioning of Lieutenant Waldheim’s role. Her
Majesty’s Government have now conducted most
thorough inquiries into the allegations concerning his role
in areas of specific British interest. The results of this
review have been recorded in a report, which [ have placed
to-day in the Library of the House, and which is being
published by HMSO.

The review has been solely concerned with areas of
British interest and has centred on the cases of British
servicemen, mostly members of the special forces,
captured, between October 1943 and October 1944 in
Greece and the Greek islands under the control of the
German Army Group E, in whose headquarters
Lieutenant Waldheim was then serving as a junior staff
officer. These cases, covering over 80 British servicemen,
include both those brought to the attention of the Ministry

of Defence and others identified during the research for ,

this review. The review has not looked at other allegations
concerning Lieutenant Waldheim’s involvement in areas
not related to British interests.

The review has involved long and painstaking research. _

The historical evidence and the documents gathered have
been examined and analysed by the director of Army legal
services who is well versed in the laws of war and who
retains a residual responsibility for the prosecution of war
crimes. His duty is to examine the evidence that has been
put before him and advise whether, on that evidence, there
is a case for a potential accused to answer. After very

careful consideration his conclusion concerning the then -

Lieutenant Waldheim jis that there is no evidence from
which guilt of a war crime might be inferred.

The report indicates that Lieutenant Waldheim knew of _

the capture of the British service men and the possible fate
of “Commandos”, but no evidence has come to light to
indicate that as a junior staff officer he had the power
either to order of to prevent that fate or indeed to affect the
outcome in any way: Knowledge in such circumstances is
not itself a crime. Her Majesty’s Government have

accepted the findings of the report.

30 CWH1/30 Job 8-1

17 OCTOBER 1989

co
Written Answers YO 60

Ne

The report also deals with British knowledge of

' Lieutenant Waldheim’s wartime role and the post-war

handling of relevant records. Allegations that records were
altered, destroyed or withheld to protect President
Waldheim are shown to be unfounded. He was not wanted
by the United Kingdom either as a “top Nazi” or as a war
criminal. In order to clarify these issues virtually all the
most relevant papers have been included as annexes to the
report. A very few documents have either not been
published or not in full, on the ground of security or
personal sensitivity. All these documents have been
examined by Professor Sir Harry Hinsley, lately the
Master of St. John’s college, Cambridge, who agreed to
scrutinise the results of the review in order to ensure its
objectivity and thoroughness. I draw the House’s attention
to his statement in the report, in which he vouches for the
validity of the conclusions drawn from these documents.
On the question of President Waldheim’s alleged post-war
involvement with foreign intelligence services and his
election to the United Nations, I do not intend to break the
practice of successive Governments by commenting on
such matters but I commend detailed consideration of the
report.

Research has concentrated on the main archives in this
country, in Germany and America, but other archives
were consulted, as well as individuals whose wartime
service was of relevance or who had some other expertise
to offer. Although it can never be said that any historic
report is definitively the last word, 1 am satisfied that the
investigation has produced, from the thousands of
documents examined, what we believe to be the main
relevant records. These have established a pattern showing
the events, and the responsibilities of the German
headquarters’ officers involved in those events. We
therefore feel justified in bringing this long exercise to a
conclusion now, and publishing the results.

A factor in this decision has been the need to consider
the feelings of the relatives of the missing service men. I
very much regret that it has not been possible to resolve all
the outstanding questions about what finally happened to
the missing service men, although I should like to assure
the House that any further information that may come to
light will be passed on to the families.

Cornfield Circles

whether any official assistance has been given by service
personnel to civilians investigating the origin of cornfield
circles in Hampshire and Wiltshire; and if he will make a

Mr. Archie Hamilton: I am not aware of any official
assistance having been given by service personnel to
civilians investigating the origin of crop circles.

. =,

Thank you for sending me a copy of Working Paper 12/93 which concerns UFO
sightings in the Ramsey area.

I received the basic details of Section 40 | Sighting shortly after the first
report was made, and have already written to him, setting out the MOD’s policy
and views on UFOs. I received one further report from the Ramsey area; although
the details vere vague, the sighting occurred in the early hours of 4 August.

I agree with your conclusion that helicopter activity seems to be the most
likely explanation. We asked RAF Alconbury whether or not they were operating
helicopters in the Benwick area at the times concerned, but they have advised
that they were not. Of course, it may well be that some other helicopter (such
as a police helicopter) was involved.

I thought it might be useful if I explained our role in relation to UFO
sightings; essentially, our only reason for being involved at all is to ensure
that there is no threat to the defence of the UK. We, together with other
specialist staffs, look at all UFO reports carefully, to look for evidence of
any such threat. To date we have found no such evidence. Most of the reports
we receive are fairly vague, and refer to little more than a vague light or
shape in the sky. Clearly there is little we can do with such reports. Where a
report is more interesting (either because of the description of the object and
its behaviour, or in cases where many witnesses are involved) we will make
further enquiries. You stated that you would normally expect JARIC to action
such a case; while I know that they could be asked to examine any interesting
UFO photographs that we may be sent, I was not aware that they had a day-to-day
role in investigating sightings. Perhaps you would let me know what the
position is.

Although I do not believe we can take any further action with regard to these
specific sightings, your report has highlighted some confusion about how UFO
reports should be handled. RAF bases, police stations and civil airports should
all have standard forms for the reporting of sightings, but the system does not
work as well as it might. This is clearly illustrated by the fact that RAF
Wyton assumed the report to be a crank call, and cut Section cae We intend to
review the existing system, and promulgate advice on the handling of UFO
reports.
