Working Paper 12/93

Aug 93
UFO OVER BENWICK, CAMBS ON 26 JUL 93
RE TED ACTION D
Introduction
1. This note records the reporting of UFO activity in the Ramsey area of Cambridge in Jul and

Aug 93. The report was actioned through official channels and formal acknowledgement of the
sighting has been made to the principal individual involved. Some possible explanations based on the
descriptions of the sighting are made.

Initial Report

2. On the morning of the 26 Jul 93 an attempt was made to report, over the telephone to RAF
Wyton, a UFO sighting. The operator took it to be a crank/hoax call and terminated the contact. The
caller then phoned RAF Brampton where the operator accepted the call and used the copy facility so
that a tape of the conversation was available for analysis.

3. The telephone report was being made, at 08:15 on the morning of 26 Jul 93, by 1 | of
Ramsey, Cambridgeshire, on behalf of his father who was not available to make the call himself. The.
report can be summarised as follows: . :

EEE senior nn <0 that he often wakes in the night. In the early

hours of the morning of 26 Jul 93 he was awake and his attention was drawn to a bright object
in the night sky seemingly a few miles to the NNE of his home in Ramsey, in the vicinity of the —
fen village of Benwick. He then found his ‘high powered German’ binoculars and focused them
on the light. For the next hour or so, accompanied by his wife, he observed the mainship
hovering. Initially 2 smaller ships came down. Little lights around the spaceship were seen.'

A full transcript of the telephone report made by HMMM is at Annex A.

4. After hearing the tape of this initial call I rang MMMM junior back to ask if he had anything
else to add and volunteered to process the inquiry. (Note that action would probably have been
requested of JARIC staff but in their absence, on stand down, the inquiry was picked up by Research
Branch, HQRAFSC.) I contacted the radar unit at RAF Wyton to see if they had any record of
anything unusual some 12-15 miles N-NNE of their location. Unfortunately the radar was not manned
at the time (03:15 to 04:45-on 26 Jul 93) so I was given a West Drayton contact. Eastern Radar of
London ATC took details from me and said they would action it through official MOD channels.
5. On the morning of the 27 Jul 93 [ spoke by telephone with EE senior and explained the
action taken. He confirmed the original details except that his version was that "2 things shot out of the.
side of it" when first seen and described it as "a saucer with lattice work". He then went on to claim
that whilst in the local doctors' waiting room on the evening of the 26th he discussed the sighting with
an elderly farm worker who was also waiting. Apparently this individual was not surprised since he

Said that an object had come down to within some 50' of him whilst he was working in Pidley village,
back in 1983. :

6. The map at Figure 1 shows Ramsey, where the sighting was made, and the village of Benwick,
the nearest habitation other than individual farms, in the area that the UFO was thought to be hovering

over.

Subsequent Report

7. One week later, 2 Aug 93 A junior phoned me to say that the sighting had been
repeated in the early hours of that morning and this time witnesses had confirmed the observation. A
possible explanation was discussed but rejected by SE junior. GERM senior had received
official acknowledgement of his sighting on the 26th, the letter apparently clearing | him to take
whatever follow up action he desired.

Possible Explanation

8. The initial description of an illuminated object hovering for an hour plus did seem difficult to
match with any man-made flying object especially with the claimed saucer shape as seen through
binoculars. However, there is a possible answer, as follows:-

The American Air Force operate out of RAF Alconbury some 15 to 20 miles south west of the
area of the sighting (see Figure 1), A current type operated is the Sikorsky HH-53 helicopter.
The range of this large rescue helicopter can be extended by external fuel tanks. Because of its
rescue role there are lights carried and hovering over an accident/incident site is a basic
requirement.

9, Consider the situation of flight training in the rescue role. The requirement is for long duration
in the hover. Given the extra tanks that can be fitted the idea of hovering for an hour plus is probably
feasible. Any combination of struts by which external tanks are attached, the nose flight refuelling
probe and the main and tail rotors could explain the ‘lattice work' comment. At night the combination
of navigation and cabin lighting would make it show up. Given the clear but windy weather in the early -
hours of the 26th the hover would not be stable and rotation, either about the single main rotor axis or
tight circling to stay over the planned ground position means that the fuselage would sweep out a shape
other than the actual helicopter fuselage shape (see Figure 2). Use of searchlights could have been
seen, at some distance, as 'projections' from the craft. The sighting was over an area of low population
density possibly to minimise noise complaints and with the windy conditions the noise may well have
been attenuated anyway. :

10. The implication that the first sighting disappeared and the second involved a rapid vertical
ascent to go out of sight are difficult to explain, but not impossible. If, in the first.case, the helicopter
departed the area heading directly away from the observers the visible lights would be minimal. In the
second case the helicopter could have used its not inconsiderable climb rate, 1000 feet per minute plus
and if it went up into the cloud base the sudden disappearance might make it seem faster than it was.
11, The helicopter solution to this sighting can be resolved if the forces at Alconbury are prepared
to admit to such training. Alternatively, the evidence of people on the ground at some of those isolated

_ fenland farms could probably confirm the noise from a very large hovering helicopter which would be
quite noticeable even if it was at a considerable height above the ground.

Conclusions

12. The coincidence of this UFO sighting happening close to a base operating the large helicopters
that could produce this impression in the sky really means its interpretation as a UFO is unlikely. Only

if the American forces deny any such operation and no one can be found to confirm the noise from such
a deployment, should this incident be taken as a truly 'Unidentified' flying object.

Res Sc la
August 1993

ANNEXATO |
. ~ WORKING PAPER NO 12/92
7 oS ATED _ UG 9

"RANSCRIPT FROM CONVERSATION RECEIVED AT RAF BRAMPTON ABC FO
I VER BE CK, CAMB 26 JUL 93 |

CALLER - They saw 2 or 3 other small little tiny ships come off the top bit, down, you know landed.
Came downwards off of this main ship towards, you know, somewhere over Ramsey or in the Fens. He
watched it for an hour and his wife did.

SWITCHBOARD - What did it actually look like, the main ship?

CALLER - You'll have to ask him. I only briefly spoke with him. He woke me up this morning all of a,
you know.

SWITCHBOARD - The main ship landed?

CALLER - The main ship hovered for an hour in the same position, okay, from 3.00 am to about 3.50
am yesterday, but to see the ship properly you had to have a pair of binoculars, okay. It just looked like
a light in the sky at the start, okay. When you look through binoculars you can see all the little lights
around the outside and windows all round the outside as well. And he said that 2 or 3 other little small
ships came off the main one and down, downwards. He didn't see where they went, he just said
downwards. So you know, I wondered if you saw anything. I rang Wyton and they just cut me off. I
thought you guys might want to know about this stuff.

- SWITCHBOARD - Yes, well it is interesting.

CALLER - He is the sanest man you will ever know, honestly. You are not talking to cranks here. We
are business people, sane people.

SWITCHBOARD - So, the main ship hovered for an hour?
CALLER - An hour.

SWITCHBOARD - And then 2 smaller ships?

CALLER - Through the naked eye it just looked like a saucer shape of light in the sky but when you
looked through binoculars at it you could see all the detail of the ship. He's got a very powerful pair of
binoculars. He lives in Ireland, you see, and he looks out to sea through his big binoculars. He looks '

through them and can see everything.

SWITCHBOARD .- Yes.

_ CALLER - He watched it from 3.15 am to 4.45 am he said.

A-l
SWITCHBOARD - What colour were the lights?

CALLER - You'll have to ask him. I don't know. I haven't really talked to him. But he's all excited. I
~ can't get over it.

SWITCHBOARD - And whereabouts in Ramsey was it?

CALLER - It wasn't actually. We are at MMM in the middle of Ramsey. He was looking
North through his back window, sort of North East, North East direction. It will be somewhere over
towards Chatteris, no not Chatteris, probably Benwick or somewhere like that. No maybe a bit further
out than that.

SWITCHBOARD - Benwick. ee

CALLER - Yes, Benwick way. It must have been a good distance for him not to see the detail of the
main craft. |

SWITCHBOARD - Yes.

CALLER - But, there you go. I'm just telling you 'cause I thought if anyone else might have saw
it...but, you know,,,Wyton didn't want to know, they cut me off.

SWITCHBOARD - Perhaps they thought you were messing about?

CALLER - Yes, but I'm saying I'm a business person. I know my father doesn't tell porkies, see. You'd
have picked them up on radar, wouldn't you?

SWITCHBOARD - Your probably quite right, yeah.

CALLER - But, there you go.

SWITCHBOARD - Thank you very much for. calling anyway.

CALLER - Alright, you know where we are, at

SWITCHBOARD - Can I take your phone number?

~ CALLER - Phone number iS And there was definitely 2 or 3 smaller ships or small

_ whatever they were came off the main ship and came down, downwards. And where they went to he
doesn't know.

SWITCHBOARD - He doesn't know whether they landed or not?

CALLER - He doesn't know where they landed, they came downwards. So, you know.

SWITCHBOARD - Can I take his name?
CALLER -
That's why he's up at that time.

A-2

SWITCHBOARD - Yes, well that's why he saw it.
CALLER - Well, there you go. This is genuine.
SWITCHBOARD - Yes, very interesting.

CALLER - I would never have believed it. I don't believe in UFOs but when your father, who you
know doesn't tell lies, doesn't exaggerate in any way, tells you, then.you thought it's got to be true.

SWITCHBOARD - Right, yes.

CALLER - Alright. | _
SWITCHBOARD - Thank you very much for calling.
CALLER - Okay. Bye, bye.

SWITCHBOARD - Okay. Bye, bye.

A-3

D/Sec(AS)12/3

Date
21 September 1993

..

Details of the report you made concerning the strange red fireball that you saw
in the sky last night have been passed to this office, as we co-ordinate reports
of UFO sightings in this country. Our only concern is to establish whether or
not there is a threat to the security of the United Kingdom. Unless we find
evidence of such a threat - and this has not been the case so far - we do not
attempt to investigate further, or to identify what was seen.

While it is clear from the reports we receive that there are many strange things
seen in the sky, we believe that explanations could be found for most of them.
T£ you do believe that what you saw cannot be explained in terms of known
objects or phenomena, you might like to contact some of the civilian
organisations currently engaged in the study and investigation of UFOs. These
organisations may be able to offer some further thoughts on what you saw, or tie
in your sighting with other reports they may have received. I suggest the
following societies:

British UFO Research Association Quest International

Contact International (UK)

 &§ no. Section 40, |

I hope this is helpful.

Yours sncewell y .

Section 40|
I had not appreciated that the pilot's sighting took place in the Sixties!
Several people mentioned to me that they had heard about a pilot reporting a UFO
in very recent media reports, and I assumed that they were talking about a
sighting that had just occurred. I do not see why an old report should have

been resurrected, unless it was being compared to a recent sighting. I would be
happy to neet SECON at sone stage.

You asked whether any papers detailing liaison between RAF and USAF personnel
might have survived. The problem is that, as I explained in my letter dated 4
August, most of our old UFO files were destroyed because it was felt that the
subject was not important enough to justify their retention. Having said this,
a few files did survive (whether this was an accident, or whether a deliberate
decision was made by a PRO archivist I do not know) and it may be that other
files survived. If they do, I am not aware of them. The only suggestion I have
is that any such papers might be on a "non-UFO file". I suspect you have
already thought of this, but I can only suggest a bit of lateral thinking,
perhaps starting with files (if there are any) covering RAF/USAF liaison.

Finally See and I were grateful for your kind invitation, and would be
delighted to join you and your girlfriend one evening. Most of October is free,
so perhaps you would give me a call so we can decide on a date.

Yous Sonerly

was there during this ertire period, and can assure you that beth the behaviour
of the UFOs, probably their numbers, and certainly the ‘response of the U, S.

repeated the Governments propaganda with no thought as to how. improbable it
sounds, The report that the government sent one airplane from an air base
in Deleware 100 miles away as its only response is a lie. The assertion that
& plane had to be sent from such @ distance because “the runway at Andrews
air force base was being repaired is obviously absurd. There are four air
force and navy air bases within 25 miles of D. C. This lie was an obvious
attempt to trivialize the event.

Thank you for your letter dated 16 September, and for sending details of a UFO

report made by your reference BM 099).

I have checked our files, and can confirm that we received no other reports that
might tie~in with this this sighting.
Given the proximity to Gatwick, and the fact that red and green flashing lights
were seen, I have to say that I think an aircraft is the most likely

explanation. The "hovering" would occur when the aircraft was heading directly
for the car, and other effects might be caused by a combination of the relative
motion of the car and aircraft, together with the fact that some distortion

might result from the object being viewed through glass. I do not find the lack
of sound surprising, and believe the other ef S could be explained. The
other point that occurs to me is cna 3 have been concentrating
on driving, so would not have been able to devote his full attention to the
object.

I wish you the best of luck with your investigation.:

Yuers swcerel

D/Sec(AS)12/3

20 September 1993

I would like to thank you for bringing in the taped account of your 1954 UFO
sighting. I must apologise again for the length of time that you were kept
waiting in the reception area; our office is the only one that deals with UFOs,
and, unfortunately, we were all out at the same time.

I have now listened to the cassette, and found what you had to say very
interesting. Unfortunately, it will probably not be possible to tie your
sighting in with others we may have received; most of our old UFO files have
been destroyed, because until the late Sixties it was not felt that there was
sufficient public interest in the subject to justify keeping the files. A few
files did survive, and coincidentally two of these cover the period 1950-54. If
you are interested in seeing these files, they are available for viewing at the
following address: The Public Record Office, Ruskin Avenue, Kew, Richmond,
Surrey, TW9 4DU. The references of the files are AIR 20/7390 and AIR 20/9994.
They will give an indication of the sorts of reports we were receiving at the
time, and might reveal reports similar to yours.

The description of the object that you saw does tie in with some reports
received over the years, and the movement - hovering, and then moving off
rapidly - is quite a common factor with UFO reports. I will certainly bear your
experience in mind when looking at fresh reports.

Once again, thank you for taking the trouble to let us know about your sighting.

LAY
Kee,

Due

Details of the report you made concerning the strange yellow lights that you saw
in the sky last night have been passed to this office, as we co-ordinate reports
of UFO sightings in this country. Our only concern is to establish whether or
not there is a threat to the security of the United Kingdom. Unless we find
evidence of such a threat ~ and this has not been the case so far - we do not
attempt to investigate further, or to identify what was seen.

While it is clear from the reports we receive that there are many strange things
seen in the sky, we believe that explanations could be found for most of them.
If you do believe that what you saw cannot be explained in terms of known
objects or phenomena, you might like to contact some of the civilian
organisations currently engaged in the study and investigation of UFOs. These
organisations may be able to offer some further thoughts on what you saw, or tie
in your sighting with other reports they may have received. I suggest the
following societies:

British UFO Research Association Quest International

Contact International (UK)

I hope this is helpful.

Thank you for your letter dated 11 September, concerning the strange ball of
light you saw.

Your letter was passed to this office as we co-ordinate reports of UFO sightings
in this country. Having said this, it sounds as if what you saw might be ball
lightning - itself very rare, and not regarded as a proven phenomenon by all of
the scientific community.

Given the above, you might like to contact the science department of your local
university, vho may well be very interested in what you sav. If they are not
interested themselves, they may well be able to suggest someone else who is.

Thank you for taking the trouble to let us know about your sighting.

TO WHOM IT MAY CONCERN

We have been advised by our local Police Station of your address, as
they felt you might be interested in the experience we had this evening
between 8.45 and 9.00 PM, in Weston Super Mare at the Bleadon Level,
Salt water on the River Axe, a member of our local Sporting Club and
myself were walking away from the sea wall towards our vehicles, when a
ball of light the size of a football (a dim not a bright light) came
straight towards us, with a rush of air traveling at a great speed, not
more than 30 yards high, just to our left, it went straight towards the
sea wall and without seeming to stop,. just came straight back towards us
again from the other way, and disappeared across the level with the same
rush of speed, another member of our club was following behind and when
we said to him, ‘you will never believe what we have just seen' he
described exactly the same experience.

I hope this will be of some interest,

n

Yours sincerely,

Date

Thank you for your letter dated 12 September.

You asked about a formation of F-lils that were seen over Shipston on 10
September; we have no record of any F-111 aircraft being in the area at the
time, although a number of other fast jet aircraft were in the area at the time.
The aircraft concerned were carrying out routine training operations.

You also asked about the collision between two Russian Mig-29 aircraft at the
International Air Tattoo at RAF Fairford. A Board of Inquiry has been called to
investigate this accident, and see whether there any lessons to learn for the
future, with a view to preventing similar accidents and further minimising the
risk to the public at air displays. This is a standard procedure with any such

accident.

Finally, you asked whether I could pass your name on to members of the public

Details of the report you made concerning the strange object that you saw in the
sky recently have been passed to this office, as we co-ordinate reports of UFO
sightings in this country. Our only concern is to establish whether or not
there is a threat to the security of the United Kingdom. Unless we find
avidence of such a threat - and this has not been the case so far - we do not
attempt to investigate further, or to identify what was seen.

While it is clear from the reports we receive that there are many strange things
seen in the sky, we believe that explanations could be found for most of them.
If you do believe that what you saw cannot be explained in terms of known
objects or phenomena, you might like to contact some of the civilian
organisations currently engaged in the study and investigation of UFOs. These
organisations may be able to offer some further thoughts on what you saw, or tie

D/Sec(AS)12/3

Thank you for your recent letter concerning Se UFO video.

You asked for some information about our policy and views on UFOs; the following
paragraphs set out our position:

While the Ministry of Defence does receive some reports of UFO sightings, our

only concern is to establish whether or not they pose a threat to the security
of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this has not been the
case so far, we do not attempt to investigate further, or to identify whatever

might have been seen.

Given this limited involvement with the. subject, the Ministry of Defence has no
department which is appointed solely for the purpose of studying UFO reports,
nor are there any staf£ employed on the subject full time. The reports we
receive, which are usually limited to very brief details of what was seen, are
passed to staff in departments concerned with the air defence of the United
Kingdom, who examine them as part of their normal duties.

It is clear from the reports we receive that there are many strange things to be
seen in the sky. However, we believe that explanations could be found for most
of them. Possibilities that spring to mind include aircraft lights or aircraft
seen from unusual angles, kites, helium balloons, weather balloons, unusual
cloud formations, satellites in orbit or satellite debris entering the
atmosphere, ball lightning, fireballs and meteorites. We accept, however, that
there will always be some sightings that defy explanation.

We will always try to be as helpful as we can in dealing with reports of UFOs,
and with more general policy questions.

I look forward to seeing SERIO £i1ms in due course, and will let both.
him and you know if I have any ideas on what they show.

Youn 4 sacead

Details of the report you made concerning the strange object that you and your
wife sav in the sky recently have been passed to this office, as we co-ordinate
reports of UFO sightings in this country. Our only concern is to establish
whether or not there is a threat to the security of the United Kingdom. Unless
we find evidence of such a threat - and this has not been the case so far - we
do not attempt to investigate further, or to identify what was seen.

While it is clear from the reports we receive that there are many strange things
seen in the sky, we believe that explanations could be found for most of them.

Thank you for your letter of 18 August on the subject of Unidentified
Flying Objects, in which you asked if there were any other reported sightings
similar to yours.

I have had a look through our sightings file, but could find no reports
similar to yours. I have checked with our Low Flying Department who have
confirmed that RAF aircraft were flying in the general area of your sighting,
although I vould agree with you that what you have reported does not sound like
an aircraft.

While we believe that most UFO sightings can be explained in terms of
known objects or phenomena, none of the more usual explanations spring to mind
in this instance. The only suggestion I have is that you may wish to check with
some of the civilian organisations currently engaged in the study and
investigations of UFOs. These organisations may be able to offer some further
thoughts on what you saw, or tie your sighting in with other reports they may
have received. I suggest the following groups:

British UFO Research Association west International

Contact International (UK)

I hope this is helpful.

Thank you for your letter dated 20 August, concerning the wave of UFO sightings
reported in the early hours of March 31 this year.

The sightings in this country occured mainly over the South-West, with most of
the reports being from Devon, Cornwall and South Wales. The most common feature
of these reports was that people reported having seen two bright white lights.
it was not clear whether these were two separate objects flying in close
formation, or whether they were lights from one object. There were conflicting
reports about the speed, height and heading. I have attached a map that I drew
up at the time, plotting the locations of sightings we know about. Where I had
the information, I added the time of the sighting (I have used British Summer
Time, ie GMT + 1 hour), and the heading of the object. As you can see, it is
all pretty confusing!

While we believe that most UFO sightings could be explained in terms of known
objects or phenomena, there will always be some that appear to defy explanation,
and we retain an open mind on these. The sightings on March 31 fall into this
category; we were not able to make a positive identification of what was seen,
and could draw no firm conclusions. This fascinating wave of sightings remains
unexplained.

I do not know whether you liaise with UFO groups in this country, but it may be
worth your while getting in touch with them to see if they have any further
data. I suggest the following groups:

British UFO Research Association Quest International

Contact International (UK)

&

Recycled Paper
I am also aware that a local researcher, affiliated to the British UFO Research
Association, made a very detailed study of these sightings, and you may wish to
contact him too. I know that believed that many of the sightings
might have been caused by the decay of a Russian rocket, and you may wish to
follow this up with him. His details are as follows:

I hope this information is helpful, and I wish you the best of luck with your
research into these intriguing sightings.

Thank you for giving SERee@permission to send me a copy of your letter to him
concerning your recent UFO sighting.

I have checked our sightings file, but I am afraid that we have received no
other reports that tie in with yours. Clearly a lot of people see something
strange, but make no report.

I am not sure what to make of this sighting. The only possible explanation that
springs to mind would be lasers or searchlights reflecting off clouds, although
not all of the details appear to fit this option. The area concerned is very
close to Blackpool though, so the possibility of the lights being caused by some
sort of equipment associated with the Illuminations should not be ruled out.
Although I understand that the Illuminations are not switched on until
September, the testing of the equipment may well have started.

Clearly this may not fit the bill, and I offer it up only as a possibility.
Although we believe that most UFO sightings can be explained in terms of known

objects or phenomena, we entirely accept that some would appear to defy
explanation, and we retain an open mind on these.

Thank you for your letter dated 16 August, in which you asked for any views we
“might have on a number of UFO sightings you are investigating.

While we believe that most UFO sightings can be explained in. terms of known
objects or phenomena, none of the more usual explanations ‘spring to mind in
this instance.

Normally I would be able to ckeck our sightings file, to see if we had received
any similar reports. Unfortunately, the earliest file we hold dates from 1985,
as we only have limited storage space, and have to send old files to the Public
Record Office, where they are covered by the 30 Year Rule.

The only suggestion I have is that you check with some of the other UFO groups
in the country, to see if they have any data. I presume you have various
contacts, but if not, I would suggest the following national groups as a
starting point: 1

British UFO Research Association Quest International

Contact International (UK)

I hope this is helpful, and I wish you the best of luck with your research.

sightings. Our only concern is to establish whether or not they pose a threat
to the security of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do - and this
has not been the case with any sighting to date - we do not attempt to
investigate further, or to identify whatever might have been seen.

seen in the sky, although we believe that explanations could be found for most

of them.

LL.

Your letter dated 5 August has been passed to this office, as we deal with
questions relating to UFOs.

Our reason for looking at UFO sightings is to check that there is no evidence of
a potential threat to the defence of the United Kingdom; we have no remit to
investigate sightings in other countries, and I suspect that official comment on
these sightings can come only from the Belgian government or Defence Ministry.
We have conducted no investigation into the sightings, and have no information

on them.

I am aware that a Belgian UFO organisation has carried out a detailed study of
the sightings concerned, and you may wish to contact them. The details are as

follows:

Societe Belge d’Etude des Phenomenes Spatiaux (SOBEPS)
i .

Belgium

I hope this is helpful, and I wish you the best of luck with your research.

Details of the report you made concerning the strange lights that you and your
neighbours saw in the sky yesterday have been passed to this office, as we
co-ordinate reports of UFO sightings. Our only concern is to establish whether
or not they pose a threat to the security of the United Kingdom. Unless we
judge that they do - and this has not been the case with any sighting to date -
we do not attempt to investigate further, or to identify whatever might have
been seen.

It is clear from the reports we receive that there are many strange things to be
seen in the sky, although we believe that explanations could be found for most
of them. In this case I suspect that the lights were caused. by powerful
searchlights, which are being used increasingly at outdoor events such as fairs
and concerts. The most common effect is four white lights, circling a small
area of the sky.

2nd August, 1993

thank you for your recent assistance with regards to the
sighting of an unusual red light by
near Kenilworth, Warwickshire.

I have interviewed this lady and she has happily provided
details regarding her sighting. I can inform you that an
independent witness living in north Warwickshire has come
forward, with a description of a very similar event, occurring
almost exactly 24 hours later. I have a number of enquiries
continuing into these two incidents of such similar appearance,
which took place in the same geographical area on consecutive
nights. However, I would ask whether you have received any
other reports of this general type from the
Warwickshire/Leicestershire area during late June or July. If
you would please let me know, I should be most grateful.

I also did as you suggested and contacted SERMOM mE on the
wave of sighting reports in the Bristol area. We have compared

notes on the sightings we have under_investigation, and last
weekend I had the opportunity to hear e Bristol
sightings and to view the video film taken by one of his
witnesses. In terms of colour, duration and behaviour, I am
satisfied that the phenomena reported from our two areas are
quite different; indeed, it seems most likely that the Bristol
cesidents were in fact watching and filming Venus.

Nevertheless, given the initial similarity and coincidental
occurrence of these events, it was only proper that I liaise
with and I thank you for your interest and trouble
in highlighting his work to me at this time.

Thank you for your attention, and I look forward to hearing
from you on this matter,

Y

I know from previous correspondence and from your books that you are familiar
with the MOD’s position on UFOs, but I thought it would be useful if I set out,
in full, our policy and views on the UFO phenomenon.

Although the Ministry of Defence does receive reports of UFO sightings, our only
concern is to establish whether or not there is any threat to the security of
the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that there is ~ and this has not been the
case with any sighting to date - we do not attempt to investigate further, or to
identify whatever might have been seen.

Given this limited involvement with the subject, the Ministry of Defence has no
department which is appointed solely for the purpose of studying UFO reports,
nor are there any staff employed on the subject full time. The reports we
receive, which are usually limited to very brief details of what was seen, are
passed to staff in departments concerned with the air defence of the United
Kingdom, who examine them as part of their normal duties.

It is clear from the reports we receive that there are many strange things to
be seen in the sky. However, we believe that explanations could be found for
most of them. Some of the possible explanations include aircraft lights or
aircraft seen from unusual angles, satellites in orbit or satellite debris
re-entering the atmosphere, high altitude kites, helium balloons, weather
balloons, searchlights or lasers reflecting off clouds, ball lightning,
fireballs and meteorites. As you are also aware, some reports turn out to be
hoaxes. Having said this, we accept that there will always be some sightings
that appear to defy explanation, and we remain open-minded about these.

The Ministry of Defence does not release material on the subject "en bloc",
because, as is the case with all government departments, all Ministry of Defence
files are subject to the Public Records Act, and generally remain closed from
public viewing for 30 years after the last action has been taken. Prior to 1967
most UFO files were routinely destroyed after about five years, because at the
time there was not perceived to be sufficient public interest in the subject to
warrant the permanent retention of this material. As you know, however, a small
number of earlier files did survive, and may be viewed at the Public Record
Office. I have had a recent look at the situation and have discovered that
because files were not destroyed immediately, surviving papers generally date
from around 1962. The reason these papers have not yet been released is that

eo

Recycled Paper
they are in Tiles where tne 1aSt enciosures die mule Leceie, ad yuu nuvw, s4ace
can only be released when the most recent enclosure is 30 years old. The good
news is that, although it is not our practice to comment in detail on material
that has not yet been released, I understand that two files (Public Record |
Office references AIR 2/16918 and AIR 2/17318) are due for release in January
1994. From then onwards there should be a steady trickle of UFO files released
each year. I suggest that you contact the Public Record Office early next year.
I have already written to the national UFO groups about this.

The files we hold in this office go back to about 1985, so we are able to answer
specific queries that people have about particular sightings which have occurred
in recent years, and can let them know whether we have a report on the incident,
and if so, what it contains. Most of the reports that we have are relatively
unexciting, and refer to little more than a vague shape or light in the sky, as
opposed to the more exotic "close encounter" reports that are sometimes written
about. I have attached for your information a copy of the form that we use to
record UFO reports.

You may be interested to know the numbers of UFO sightings reported to the
Ministry of Defence in the past, so I have attached some figures that you may
find useful, together with some maps which give the approximate location of
recent year’s sightings.

A number of recent reports were almost certainly sightings of an airship -
illuminated from the inside. I have seen this myself, and can understand how,
when seen from a digtance, this generated reports of a bright cigar-shaped UFO.
Other recent reports have been caused by the use of lasers or searchlights; such
equipment is used increasingly in advertising and entertainment, especially at
outdoor music festivals.

The Ministry of Defence is not involved in any research or investigation into
the crop circle phenomenon, although we occasionally get questions on this
subject, because of the perceived link between crop circles and the UFO
phenomenon. Although having no responsibility for this subject, we will refer
anybody who asks about them to organisations such as CPR International or the
CCCS, in the same way as we will refer people who report or enquire about UFOs
to groups such as BUFORA or Quest International. As is the case with UFOs, we
hold no fixed views on crop circles, and remain open-minded about this
intriguing phenomenon. :

I hope this is useful, and has helped explain our position.

Vener f sty ,
